What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, yeah. So, um, the main thing that pushed me to come in is... well, I work as an insurance claims clerk. It’s a lot of policy processing and stuff. Honestly, the office is absolutely freezing right now. I don’t know why they keep the AC cranked so high, especially since it's barely even warm outside. My buddy Dave in accounting actually brought in a little space heater last week, but HR made him take it home because they said it was a fire hazard. Which is pretty funny because the microwave in the second-floor breakroom literally sparks if you run it for more than two minutes. I tried to heat up a burrito in there on Tuesday and thought I was gonna burn the building down. \n\nBut, um, anyway... yeah, I’m here because I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day. Like, I’ll just be wandering around the office even when I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. I just can't seem to stay in my chair when I need to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless when you're sitting at your workstation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess you could say that. Like, if I'm stuck in one of those long team meetings or we have to do a training session, I'm always bouncing my leg or clicking my pen. Just shifting around in my chair a lot, even when I'm really trying to look like I'm paying attention. Actually, my pen clicking drives my supervisor crazy. She uses these really nice gel pens, the kind that don't click, they just have the little cap. I tried using one of those once, but I ended up chewing on the cap until it cracked and got ink all over my hands. It took like three days to wash off. I had this huge blue smudge on my thumb when I went to my cousin's wedding that weekend. But yeah, I just can't seem to sit totally still.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless like this outside of work as well?\n\nPatient: Um, outside of work? Not really, no. I mean, I don't think so. I usually just crash when I get home. My apartment is pretty small, honestly. It's on the third floor of this building that used to be an old shoe factory, which sounds cool, but the insulation is terrible. My neighbor plays the bass guitar at like 11 PM on Tuesdays. Why Tuesdays? I have no idea. I ran into him in the hall once and he was carrying this massive bag of dog food, but I've never heard a dog in his place. Just the bass. It vibrates right through the wall where my TV is. But yeah, no, I don't really feel that restless feeling when I'm not at the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're sitting at your workstation?\n\nPatient: Anxious? No, I wouldn't say anxious. It's not like I'm worried about anything specific, you know? Well, except maybe for the vending machine in the breakroom. They restocked it last week with those jalapeno chips I like, but the coil on the C4 slot is totally busted. I watched this guy from marketing lose a dollar-fifty to it on Monday. He just stood there staring at it for like five minutes. Honestly, I thought he was going to shake the machine, which they explicitly tell you not to do in the employee handbook. They actually made us read the whole handbook out loud during orientation. It took like three hours. The guy reading before me had this really intense monotone voice, kind of like that guy from those old Ferris Bueller movies. But, um, yeah... not anxious. Just... I don't know, antsy? Like my body just wants to move.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to get up and move around when you're sitting at your workstation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's... that's the main thing that pushed me to come in here, honestly. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day. Like, I know I'm supposed to stay put and just finish my claim reviews—they track our metrics pretty closely, you know? But I'll just find myself getting up to go to the breakroom or the bathroom or just walking down the hall. \n\nOur hallway has this really weird carpet, by the way. It's this crazy geometric pattern from the 90s, all teal and purple and gray. It looks exactly like the seats on a charter bus I took to Washington D.C. for a field trip in eighth grade. I actually lost my disposable camera on that trip, which really sucked because I took a picture of a squirrel eating an entire slice of pepperoni pizza outside the Smithsonian. It was dragging it up a tree and everything. I always wondered if someone found that camera and developed the film and just got really confused. \n\nBut yeah, um... what was the question? Oh, getting up. Yeah, I just can't seem to stay in my chair to do my claim reviews. I'm always finding a reason to get up and wander off.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to get up and move around at specific times or in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Um, specific times? Not really. It's just kind of all day. I don't feel like I have to move in a certain way, either. It's just this urge to not be sitting anymore. I mean, my chair is actually pretty comfortable. It's one of those ergonomic mesh ones. My buddy Dave has the same one in his home office, but he got his at a discount office supply place that was going out of business. He said the guy running the register was wearing a full tuxedo, which is hilarious for a Tuesday afternoon in a strip mall. Dave is always finding weird stuff like that. He once bought a vintage gumball machine at a garage sale and filled it with Skittles, but they got all sticky in the summer heat and jammed the whole mechanism. \n\nBut yeah, no, there's no specific routine or time or anything. I just... I just have to get up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your workstation because you absolutely have to, or is it more that you just really want to?\n\nPatient: I don't know if \"absolutely have to\" is the right way to put it. It's not like a life-or-death feeling, you know? It's more like... I just really, really want to. Like this itch in my brain that says \"okay, time to stand up now.\" It's kind of like when you're watching a movie and you realize you forgot to take the laundry out of the dryer, and you know it's going to get all wrinkled if you don't do it right then. \n\nSpeaking of laundry, I tried this new detergent last week. It was supposed to smell like \"Mountain Rain\" or something, but it honestly just smells like those little pine tree air fresheners people hang in their cars. My dad used to have like five of those hanging from his rearview mirror at once in his old Chevy truck. He refused to get rid of that truck, even when the passenger side door wouldn't open from the inside anymore. You had to roll down the window and use the outside handle if you wanted to get out. We took it to a drive-in movie theater once, which was super cool. Have you ever been to a drive-in? There's hardly any left around here. They always had the best popcorn, way better than regular theaters. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, getting up from my desk. Yeah, I wouldn't say I *have* to, like I'm being forced. I just really want to, and then before I know it, I'm already walking away from my desk.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your workstation to do something specific, or is it just to move around in general?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really to do anything specific. I just kind of... walk. Like, I don't have a destination in mind most of the time. I'll just end up wandering over by the big windows near the elevators. They're those massive floor-to-ceiling ones, you know? They actually had to put these little frosted bird silhouettes on them a few weeks ago because birds kept flying straight into the glass. It was happening like twice a week. Which is super sad, obviously, but the sound it makes is surprisingly loud. It echoes through the whole floor. \n\nMy grandma used to have this parakeet named Petey when I was a kid, and he would always fly right into her sliding glass door if she forgot to close the curtains. He was usually fine, just kind of dazed, but he loved eating those little millet spray things... man, those things make a huge mess. She was always vacuuming birdseed off the rug. She had this really old Hoover vacuum that weighed like fifty pounds and sounded like a jet engine taking off. \n\nBut, uh... yeah, I don't get up to do a task or anything. Just to move around, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you can't control?\n\nPatient: I mean, I *can* control it, I think. It's not like my legs move on their own or anything. It's just... really hard not to. It feels like if I try to force myself to stay seated, the urge just gets stronger and stronger until I finally give in. It's sort of like when you're trying to hold your breath underwater. \n\nI used to do that all the time when I was little at the community pool. We'd have these contests to see who could stay under the longest. My friend Tyler always won because he took swimming lessons at the Y. He even learned how to do that flip turn thing at the end of the lane. I tried it once and ended up just kicking the wall super hard and bruising my heel. I couldn't wear regular shoes for like a week, I had to wear these weird foam sandals my mom bought at the pharmacy. They were bright orange, which was super embarrassing for a middle schooler. The pharmacy we went to was one of those old-school ones that still had a soda fountain in the back, though. They made the best cherry phosphates. \n\nBut yeah, wait, control. Right. I wouldn't say I *can't* control it, it's just... really uncomfortable if I try to ignore it, so I just end up getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you feel compelled to, rather than just because you want to?\n\nPatient: I guess \"compelled\" is probably a good word for it. Honestly, that's actually the main thing that pushed me to come in to see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, over and over, even when I know I’m absolutely supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI process insurance policies and claims, and man, some of the stuff people submit is wild. Just the other day I had a claim where a guy said a wild turkey shattered his windshield. A whole turkey! I didn't even know turkeys could fly that high. I always thought they just kind of hopped around on the ground, like chickens. My uncle had chickens when I was growing up—Rhode Island Reds, I think they were called. They laid these really cool brown eggs, and my aunt used to make the best quiche out of them. She’d put spinach and this really fancy gruyere cheese in it. \n\nI tried to make a quiche myself once a couple years ago, but I didn't blind-bake the crust first, so it got super soggy on the bottom. Have you ever watched the Great British Baking Show? They always talk about getting a \"soggy bottom.\" Paul Hollywood would have been so disappointed in me. It's such a relaxing show to have on in the background, though. The tent they bake in just looks so nice, especially when it's raining outside. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, feeling compelled. Yeah, I know I need to sit there and do the reviews, but I just keep getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your desk out of habit or because you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's a bit of both, but mostly it feels like I just have to. Like I said before, that's really the whole reason I finally made an appointment. I just... I keep getting up from my desk. All day. Even when I have a stack of claim reviews that absolutely have to get done, and I know I'm supposed to just stay put and finish them. \n\nIt's actually a pretty strict metric we have to hit for the reviews, they track our numbers every week. My supervisor, Brenda, she sends out this spreadsheet every Friday afternoon. She color-codes it, too, which I always thought was a little passive-aggressive. Like, if you're in the red, everyone knows. She uses this really bright, aggressive red, too, not like a soft pink or anything. It reminds me of my old high school English teacher, Mr. Harrison. He used to grade our essays with this thick red marker that bled through the paper. He was a tough grader, but he actually gave me a B+ on my final paper about *The Great Gatsby*. I always liked that book. The whole green light at the end of the dock thing... it's just really good symbolism, you know? They made a movie out of it a few years ago with Leonardo DiCaprio. He was really good in it, but I think I prefer him in *Inception*. That movie completely messed with my head. The spinning top at the end? So frustrating. I watched it three times trying to figure out if he was still dreaming or not. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... yeah. It's not just a habit. I know I'm supposed to stay at my workstation and do the work, but I just keep getting up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any specific thoughts or urges in your mind when you feel the need to get up from your desk?\n\nPatient: Not really any specific thoughts, no. Like, I'm not thinking \"I need to get up to go check the mail\" or \"I need to get up to stretch.\" It's just... this physical restlessness. And like I was saying, that's really the main thing that pushed me to come in. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI've tried bringing a water bottle to my desk so I don't have an excuse to go to the breakroom, but then I just end up getting up to go to the bathroom more often. I bought one of those really big insulated ones, the kind that holds like half a gallon. It's blue, like a really bright cobalt blue. I dropped it in the parking lot last week and dented the bottom, which is super annoying because now it wobbles when I set it down. It makes this metallic clinking sound every time I bump my desk. My desk is one of those standing desks, actually, but the motor broke on it like three months ago so it's stuck in the sitting position. Maintenance keeps saying they're going to come fix it, but you know how that goes. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always telling these really long stories about his RV trips. He went to Mount Rushmore last summer and apparently got a flat tire right in front of George Washington's face. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really have a specific thought in my head when it happens. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there doing my reviews, and I just can't seem to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk makes you feel better or more comfortable?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe a little bit? It's not like it's a huge relief or anything, but it does relieve that restless feeling for a second. But honestly, like I've been saying, it's just really frustrating. It's the whole reason I'm here. I just keep getting up from my workstation, over and over, even when I know I have to stay put and get those claim reviews done. \n\nIt's actually kind of embarrassing when other people notice. My coworker, Sarah, sits in the cubicle next to mine. She has this huge collection of succulents on her desk. Like, an aggressive amount of plants. I don't know how she keeps them all alive, especially with the weird fluorescent lighting in our office. I bought a cactus once from IKEA and managed to kill it in a month. I think I overwatered it. Did you know you're only supposed to water them like once a month? I was giving it a little drink every day like it was a regular houseplant. It just kind of turned to mush. IKEA has really good Swedish meatballs, though. I usually go just to eat at the cafeteria and don't even buy any furniture. The lingonberry jam is surprisingly good with the gravy. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if getting up makes me feel *better*, exactly. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there working, and I can't.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you would feel anxious or uncomfortable if you didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do feel a little uncomfortable if I try to force myself to stay sitting. It's like this tension builds up. But honestly, like I keep saying, this is exactly what pushed me to come see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even though I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nIt’s just so hard to focus when my chair is so uncomfortable, too. It’s one of those mesh ones, but the lumbar support piece snapped off. I tried taping it back on with duct tape—the silver kind, not the black kind. Did you know they make duct tape in all sorts of patterns now? I saw some at the hardware store the other day that looked like galaxy print. My niece would love that, she's obsessed with space right now. She wants to be an astronaut. Or a veterinarian for space dogs, I'm not really sure which. She's only six, so her career plans change every week. Last week she wanted to be a mermaid princess. We watched *The Little Mermaid* together, the animated one, not the new live-action one. The music in that movie is just so catchy. \"Under the Sea\" was stuck in my head for three days straight. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I do feel uncomfortable if I don't get up, but the main issue is just that I keep doing it when I really need to be working.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean, maybe a little? But having the work there definitely makes it worse. Like, if I see a file that needs a long written explanation or a bunch of policy language checked, I’ll just put it off. I’ll do all the easier, quick tasks until I’m basically out of time for the day, and I end up getting up from my desk a lot in between. 

Those quick tasks are usually just, like, clicking a few boxes or approving standard stuff. There was this one file last week where a guy backed his boat trailer into a drive-thru menu board at a fast food place. Which is crazy, right? Who takes a whole boat through a drive-thru? It was a Taco Bell, I think. I haven't had Taco Bell in ages. They brought back the Mexican Pizza recently, though. My roommate was so hyped about it, he ordered like three of them on DoorDash. DoorDash is getting so expensive now with all the fees. You order a ten-dollar meal and somehow it costs twenty-five bucks by the time you check out. Plus the tip. I always try to tip well because I used to deliver pizzas when I was taking classes at the community college. My car smelled like pepperoni and cardboard for a solid two years. It was an old Honda Civic. Great gas mileage, but the AC was completely shot, so in the summer I just had to roll the windows down and sweat it out... 

Wait, what was the question? Oh, right, getting up from the desk. But yeah, if I have a file that needs a lot of actual thinking or typing out long explanations, I just avoid it and do the easy ones until the day is almost over.

He once bought a vintage gumball machine at a garage sale and filled it with Skittles, but they got all sticky in the summer heat and jammed the whole mechanism. \n\nBut yeah, no, there's no specific routine or time or anything. I just... I just have to get up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your workstation because you absolutely have to, or is it more that you just really want to?\n\nPatient: I don't know if \"absolutely have to\" is the right way to put it. It's not like a life-or-death feeling, you know? It's more like... I just really, really want to. Like this itch in my brain that says \"okay, time to stand up now.\" It's kind of like when you're watching a movie and you realize you forgot to take the laundry out of the dryer, and you know it's going to get all wrinkled if you don't do it right then. \n\nSpeaking of laundry, I tried this new detergent last week. It was supposed to smell like \"Mountain Rain\" or something, but it honestly just smells like those little pine tree air fresheners people hang in their cars. My dad used to have like five of those hanging from his rearview mirror at once in his old Chevy truck. He refused to get rid of that truck, even when the passenger side door wouldn't open from the inside anymore. You had to roll down the window and use the outside handle if you wanted to get out. We took it to a drive-in movie theater once, which was super cool. Have you ever been to a drive-in? There's hardly any left around here. They always had the best popcorn, way better than regular theaters. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, getting up from my desk. Yeah, I wouldn't say I *have* to, like I'm being forced. I just really want to, and then before I know it, I'm already walking away from my desk.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your workstation to do something specific, or is it just to move around in general?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really to do anything specific. I just kind of... walk. Like, I don't have a destination in mind most of the time. I'll just end up wandering over by the big windows near the elevators. They're those massive floor-to-ceiling ones, you know? They actually had to put these little frosted bird silhouettes on them a few weeks ago because birds kept flying straight into the glass. It was happening like twice a week. Which is super sad, obviously, but the sound it makes is surprisingly loud. It echoes through the whole floor. \n\nMy grandma used to have this parakeet named Petey when I was a kid, and he would always fly right into her sliding glass door if she forgot to close the curtains. He was usually fine, just kind of dazed, but he loved eating those little millet spray things... man, those things make a huge mess. She was always vacuuming birdseed off the rug. She had this really old Hoover vacuum that weighed like fifty pounds and sounded like a jet engine taking off. \n\nBut, uh... yeah, I don't get up to do a task or anything. Just to move around, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you can't control?\n\nPatient: I mean, I *can* control it, I think. It's not like my legs move on their own or anything. It's just... really hard not to. It feels like if I try to force myself to stay seated, the urge just gets stronger and stronger until I finally give in. It's sort of like when you're trying to hold your breath underwater. \n\nI used to do that all the time when I was little at the community pool. We'd have these contests to see who could stay under the longest. My friend Tyler always won because he took swimming lessons at the Y. He even learned how to do that flip turn thing at the end of the lane. I tried it once and ended up just kicking the wall super hard and bruising my heel. I couldn't wear regular shoes for like a week, I had to wear these weird foam sandals my mom bought at the pharmacy. They were bright orange, which was super embarrassing for a middle schooler. The pharmacy we went to was one of those old-school ones that still had a soda fountain in the back, though. They made the best cherry phosphates. \n\nBut yeah, wait, control. Right. I wouldn't say I *can't* control it, it's just... really uncomfortable if I try to ignore it, so I just end up getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you feel compelled to, rather than just because you want to?\n\nPatient: I guess \"compelled\" is probably a good word for it. Honestly, that's actually the main thing that pushed me to come in to see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, over and over, even when I know I’m absolutely supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI process insurance policies and claims, and man, some of the stuff people submit is wild. Just the other day I had a claim where a guy said a wild turkey shattered his windshield. A whole turkey! I didn't even know turkeys could fly that high. I always thought they just kind of hopped around on the ground, like chickens. My uncle had chickens when I was growing up—Rhode Island Reds, I think they were called. They laid these really cool brown eggs, and my aunt used to make the best quiche out of them. She’d put spinach and this really fancy gruyere cheese in it. \n\nI tried to make a quiche myself once a couple years ago, but I didn't blind-bake the crust first, so it got super soggy on the bottom. Have you ever watched the Great British Baking Show? They always talk about getting a \"soggy bottom.\" Paul Hollywood would have been so disappointed in me. It's such a relaxing show to have on in the background, though. The tent they bake in just looks so nice, especially when it's raining outside. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, feeling compelled. Yeah, I know I need to sit there and do the reviews, but I just keep getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your desk out of habit or because you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's a bit of both, but mostly it feels like I just have to. Like I said before, that's really the whole reason I finally made an appointment. I just... I keep getting up from my desk. All day. Even when I have a stack of claim reviews that absolutely have to get done, and I know I'm supposed to just stay put and finish them. \n\nIt's actually a pretty strict metric we have to hit for the reviews, they track our numbers every week. My supervisor, Brenda, she sends out this spreadsheet every Friday afternoon. She color-codes it, too, which I always thought was a little passive-aggressive. Like, if you're in the red, everyone knows. She uses this really bright, aggressive red, too, not like a soft pink or anything. It reminds me of my old high school English teacher, Mr. Harrison. He used to grade our essays with this thick red marker that bled through the paper. He was a tough grader, but he actually gave me a B+ on my final paper about *The Great Gatsby*. I always liked that book. The whole green light at the end of the dock thing... it's just really good symbolism, you know? They made a movie out of it a few years ago with Leonardo DiCaprio. He was really good in it, but I think I prefer him in *Inception*. That movie completely messed with my head. The spinning top at the end? So frustrating. I watched it three times trying to figure out if he was still dreaming or not. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... yeah. It's not just a habit. I know I'm supposed to stay at my workstation and do the work, but I just keep getting up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any specific thoughts or urges in your mind when you feel the need to get up from your desk?\n\nPatient: Not really any specific thoughts, no. Like, I'm not thinking \"I need to get up to go check the mail\" or \"I need to get up to stretch.\" It's just... this physical restlessness. And like I was saying, that's really the main thing that pushed me to come in. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI've tried bringing a water bottle to my desk so I don't have an excuse to go to the breakroom, but then I just end up getting up to go to the bathroom more often. I bought one of those really big insulated ones, the kind that holds like half a gallon. It's blue, like a really bright cobalt blue. I dropped it in the parking lot last week and dented the bottom, which is super annoying because now it wobbles when I set it down. It makes this metallic clinking sound every time I bump my desk. My desk is one of those standing desks, actually, but the motor broke on it like three months ago so it's stuck in the sitting position. Maintenance keeps saying they're going to come fix it, but you know how that goes. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always telling these really long stories about his RV trips. He went to Mount Rushmore last summer and apparently got a flat tire right in front of George Washington's face. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really have a specific thought in my head when it happens. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there doing my reviews, and I just can't seem to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk makes you feel better or more comfortable?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe a little bit? It's not like it's a huge relief or anything, but it does relieve that restless feeling for a second. But honestly, like I've been saying, it's just really frustrating. It's the whole reason I'm here. I just keep getting up from my workstation, over and over, even when I know I have to stay put and get those claim reviews done. \n\nIt's actually kind of embarrassing when other people notice. My coworker, Sarah, sits in the cubicle next to mine. She has this huge collection of succulents on her desk. Like, an aggressive amount of plants. I don't know how she keeps them all alive, especially with the weird fluorescent lighting in our office. I bought a cactus once from IKEA and managed to kill it in a month. I think I overwatered it. Did you know you're only supposed to water them like once a month? I was giving it a little drink every day like it was a regular houseplant. It just kind of turned to mush. IKEA has really good Swedish meatballs, though. I usually go just to eat at the cafeteria and don't even buy any furniture. The lingonberry jam is surprisingly good with the gravy. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if getting up makes me feel *better*, exactly. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there working, and I can't.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you would feel anxious or uncomfortable if you didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do feel a little uncomfortable if I try to force myself to stay sitting. It's like this tension builds up. But honestly, like I keep saying, this is exactly what pushed me to come see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even though I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nIt’s just so hard to focus when my chair is so uncomfortable, too. It’s one of those mesh ones, but the lumbar support piece snapped off. I tried taping it back on with duct tape—the silver kind, not the black kind. Did you know they make duct tape in all sorts of patterns now? I saw some at the hardware store the other day that looked like galaxy print. My niece would love that, she's obsessed with space right now. She wants to be an astronaut. Or a veterinarian for space dogs, I'm not really sure which. She's only six, so her career plans change every week. Last week she wanted to be a mermaid princess. We watched *The Little Mermaid* together, the animated one, not the new live-action one. The music in that movie is just so catchy. \"Under the Sea\" was stuck in my head for three days straight. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I do feel uncomfortable if I don't get up, but the main issue is just that I keep doing it when I really need to be working.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still get up from your desk even if you didn't have any claim reviews or work tasks to avoid?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe a little? But having the work there definitely makes it worse. Like, if I see a file that needs a long written explanation or a bunch of policy language checked, I’ll just put it off. I’ll do all the easier, quick tasks until I’m basically out of time for the day, and I end up getting up from my desk a lot in between. \n\nThose quick tasks are usually just, like, clicking a few boxes or approving standard stuff. There was this one file last week where a guy backed his boat trailer into a drive-thru menu board at a fast food place. Which is crazy, right? Who takes a whole boat through a drive-thru? It was a Taco Bell, I think. I haven't had Taco Bell in ages. They brought back the Mexican Pizza recently, though. My roommate was so hyped about it, he ordered like three of them on DoorDash. DoorDash is getting so expensive now with all the fees. You order a ten-dollar meal and somehow it costs twenty-five bucks by the time you check out. Plus the tip. I always try to tip well because I used to deliver pizzas when I was taking classes at the community college. My car smelled like pepperoni and cardboard for a solid two years. It was an old Honda Civic. Great gas mileage, but the AC was completely shot, so in the summer I just had to roll the windows down and sweat it out... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right, getting up from the desk. But yeah, if I have a file that needs a lot of actual thinking or typing out long explanations, I just avoid it and do the easy ones until the day is almost over.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don't know if "compelling" is the right word. It's not like a voice in my head telling me to stand up or anything like that. But honestly, like I keep saying, this is the main thing that pushed me to come in. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I'm supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. 

It's just so frustrating because I'll look at the clock and realize I've been wandering around the office for ten minutes. The break room is all the way on the other side of the floor, so it takes a while to get there. They have one of those fancy coffee machines now, the ones that grind the beans right in front of you. It's so loud, though. Sounds like a jet engine taking off. My buddy works at the airport, actually. He's one of those guys who waves the light wands to guide the planes in. He says it's pretty cool, but he has to wear those heavy-duty ear muffs all day. I don't think I could do that. I like listening to podcasts while I work, when I can actually focus on them. I've been super into this true crime one lately about art heists. It's crazy how people can just walk out of a museum with a painting worth millions of dollars. Like, don't they have security cameras? I bought a cheap security camera off Amazon for my apartment once because I thought someone was stealing my Amazon packages. Turned out it was just the delivery guy leaving them at the wrong door down the hall. My neighbor was so confused when he kept finding boxes of protein powder and cat food on his welcome mat. I don't even have a cat. I just buy the food to feed the strays behind my building...

But yeah, I don't feel forced to get up, exactly. I just do it, even when I know I really need to stay in my seat and work.

Like this itch in my brain that says \"okay, time to stand up now.\" It's kind of like when you're watching a movie and you realize you forgot to take the laundry out of the dryer, and you know it's going to get all wrinkled if you don't do it right then. \n\nSpeaking of laundry, I tried this new detergent last week. My dad used to have like five of those hanging from his rearview mirror at once in his old Chevy truck. He refused to get rid of that truck, even when the passenger side door wouldn't open from the inside anymore. You had to roll down the window and use the outside handle if you wanted to get out. We took it to a drive-in movie theater once, which was super cool. Have you ever been to a drive-in? There's hardly any left around here. They're those massive floor-to-ceiling ones, you know? They actually had to put these little frosted bird silhouettes on them a few weeks ago because birds kept flying straight into the glass. It was happening like twice a week. Which is super sad, obviously, but the sound it makes is surprisingly loud. It echoes through the whole floor. \n\nMy grandma used to have this parakeet named Petey when I was a kid, and he would always fly right into her sliding glass door if she forgot to close the curtains. He was usually fine, just kind of dazed, but he loved eating those little millet spray things... man, those things make a huge mess. She was always vacuuming birdseed off the rug. She had this really old Hoover vacuum that weighed like fifty pounds and sounded like a jet engine taking off. \n\nBut, uh... yeah, I don't get up to do a task or anything. Just to move around, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you can't control?\n\nPatient: I mean, I *can* control it, I think. It's not like my legs move on their own or anything. It's just... really hard not to. It feels like if I try to force myself to stay seated, the urge just gets stronger and stronger until I finally give in. It's sort of like when you're trying to hold your breath underwater. \n\nI used to do that all the time when I was little at the community pool. We'd have these contests to see who could stay under the longest. My friend Tyler always won because he took swimming lessons at the Y. He even learned how to do that flip turn thing at the end of the lane. I tried it once and ended up just kicking the wall super hard and bruising my heel. I couldn't wear regular shoes for like a week, I had to wear these weird foam sandals my mom bought at the pharmacy. They were bright orange, which was super embarrassing for a middle schooler. The pharmacy we went to was one of those old-school ones that still had a soda fountain in the back, though. They made the best cherry phosphates. \n\nBut yeah, wait, control. Right. I wouldn't say I *can't* control it, it's just... really uncomfortable if I try to ignore it, so I just end up getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you feel compelled to, rather than just because you want to?\n\nPatient: I guess \"compelled\" is probably a good word for it. Honestly, that's actually the main thing that pushed me to come in to see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, over and over, even when I know I’m absolutely supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI process insurance policies and claims, and man, some of the stuff people submit is wild. Just the other day I had a claim where a guy said a wild turkey shattered his windshield. A whole turkey! I didn't even know turkeys could fly that high. I always thought they just kind of hopped around on the ground, like chickens. My uncle had chickens when I was growing up—Rhode Island Reds, I think they were called. They laid these really cool brown eggs, and my aunt used to make the best quiche out of them. She’d put spinach and this really fancy gruyere cheese in it. \n\nI tried to make a quiche myself once a couple years ago, but I didn't blind-bake the crust first, so it got super soggy on the bottom. Have you ever watched the Great British Baking Show? They always talk about getting a \"soggy bottom.\" Paul Hollywood would have been so disappointed in me. It's such a relaxing show to have on in the background, though. The tent they bake in just looks so nice, especially when it's raining outside. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, feeling compelled. Yeah, I know I need to sit there and do the reviews, but I just keep getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your desk out of habit or because you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's a bit of both, but mostly it feels like I just have to. Like I said before, that's really the whole reason I finally made an appointment. I just... I keep getting up from my desk. All day. Even when I have a stack of claim reviews that absolutely have to get done, and I know I'm supposed to just stay put and finish them. \n\nIt's actually a pretty strict metric we have to hit for the reviews, they track our numbers every week. My supervisor, Brenda, she sends out this spreadsheet every Friday afternoon. She color-codes it, too, which I always thought was a little passive-aggressive. Like, if you're in the red, everyone knows. She uses this really bright, aggressive red, too, not like a soft pink or anything. It reminds me of my old high school English teacher, Mr. Harrison. He used to grade our essays with this thick red marker that bled through the paper. He was a tough grader, but he actually gave me a B+ on my final paper about *The Great Gatsby*. I always liked that book. The whole green light at the end of the dock thing... it's just really good symbolism, you know? They made a movie out of it a few years ago with Leonardo DiCaprio. He was really good in it, but I think I prefer him in *Inception*. That movie completely messed with my head. The spinning top at the end? So frustrating. I watched it three times trying to figure out if he was still dreaming or not. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... yeah. It's not just a habit. I know I'm supposed to stay at my workstation and do the work, but I just keep getting up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any specific thoughts or urges in your mind when you feel the need to get up from your desk?\n\nPatient: Not really any specific thoughts, no. Like, I'm not thinking \"I need to get up to go check the mail\" or \"I need to get up to stretch.\" It's just... this physical restlessness. And like I was saying, that's really the main thing that pushed me to come in. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI've tried bringing a water bottle to my desk so I don't have an excuse to go to the breakroom, but then I just end up getting up to go to the bathroom more often. I bought one of those really big insulated ones, the kind that holds like half a gallon. It's blue, like a really bright cobalt blue. I dropped it in the parking lot last week and dented the bottom, which is super annoying because now it wobbles when I set it down. It makes this metallic clinking sound every time I bump my desk. My desk is one of those standing desks, actually, but the motor broke on it like three months ago so it's stuck in the sitting position. Maintenance keeps saying they're going to come fix it, but you know how that goes. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always telling these really long stories about his RV trips. He went to Mount Rushmore last summer and apparently got a flat tire right in front of George Washington's face. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really have a specific thought in my head when it happens. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there doing my reviews, and I just can't seem to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk makes you feel better or more comfortable?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe a little bit? It's not like it's a huge relief or anything, but it does relieve that restless feeling for a second. But honestly, like I've been saying, it's just really frustrating. It's the whole reason I'm here. I just keep getting up from my workstation, over and over, even when I know I have to stay put and get those claim reviews done. \n\nIt's actually kind of embarrassing when other people notice. My coworker, Sarah, sits in the cubicle next to mine. She has this huge collection of succulents on her desk. Like, an aggressive amount of plants. I don't know how she keeps them all alive, especially with the weird fluorescent lighting in our office. I bought a cactus once from IKEA and managed to kill it in a month. I think I overwatered it. Did you know you're only supposed to water them like once a month? I was giving it a little drink every day like it was a regular houseplant. It just kind of turned to mush. IKEA has really good Swedish meatballs, though. I usually go just to eat at the cafeteria and don't even buy any furniture. The lingonberry jam is surprisingly good with the gravy. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if getting up makes me feel *better*, exactly. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there working, and I can't.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you would feel anxious or uncomfortable if you didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do feel a little uncomfortable if I try to force myself to stay sitting. It's like this tension builds up. But honestly, like I keep saying, this is exactly what pushed me to come see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even though I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nIt’s just so hard to focus when my chair is so uncomfortable, too. It’s one of those mesh ones, but the lumbar support piece snapped off. I tried taping it back on with duct tape—the silver kind, not the black kind. Did you know they make duct tape in all sorts of patterns now? I saw some at the hardware store the other day that looked like galaxy print. My niece would love that, she's obsessed with space right now. She wants to be an astronaut. Or a veterinarian for space dogs, I'm not really sure which. She's only six, so her career plans change every week. Last week she wanted to be a mermaid princess. We watched *The Little Mermaid* together, the animated one, not the new live-action one. The music in that movie is just so catchy. \"Under the Sea\" was stuck in my head for three days straight. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... I do feel uncomfortable if I don't get up, but the main issue is just that I keep doing it when I really need to be working.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still get up from your desk even if you didn't have any claim reviews or work tasks to avoid?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe a little? But having the work there definitely makes it worse. Like, if I see a file that needs a long written explanation or a bunch of policy language checked, I’ll just put it off. I’ll do all the easier, quick tasks until I’m basically out of time for the day, and I end up getting up from my desk a lot in between. \n\nThose quick tasks are usually just, like, clicking a few boxes or approving standard stuff. There was this one file last week where a guy backed his boat trailer into a drive-thru menu board at a fast food place. Which is crazy, right? Who takes a whole boat through a drive-thru? It was a Taco Bell, I think. I haven't had Taco Bell in ages. They brought back the Mexican Pizza recently, though. My roommate was so hyped about it, he ordered like three of them on DoorDash. DoorDash is getting so expensive now with all the fees. You order a ten-dollar meal and somehow it costs twenty-five bucks by the time you check out. Plus the tip. I always try to tip well because I used to deliver pizzas when I was taking classes at the community college. My car smelled like pepperoni and cardboard for a solid two years. It was an old Honda Civic. Great gas mileage, but the AC was completely shot, so in the summer I just had to roll the windows down and sweat it out... \n\nWait, what was the question? Oh, right, getting up from the desk. But yeah, if I have a file that needs a lot of actual thinking or typing out long explanations, I just avoid it and do the easy ones until the day is almost over.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your desk because something is compelling you to do so, even if you don't want to?\n\nPatient: I don't know if \"compelling\" is the right word. It's not like a voice in my head telling me to stand up or anything like that. But honestly, like I keep saying, this is the main thing that pushed me to come in. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I'm supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nIt's just so frustrating because I'll look at the clock and realize I've been wandering around the office for ten minutes. The break room is all the way on the other side of the floor, so it takes a while to get there. They have one of those fancy coffee machines now, the ones that grind the beans right in front of you. It's so loud, though. Sounds like a jet engine taking off. My buddy works at the airport, actually. He's one of those guys who waves the light wands to guide the planes in. He says it's pretty cool, but he has to wear those heavy-duty ear muffs all day. I don't think I could do that. I like listening to podcasts while I work, when I can actually focus on them. I've been super into this true crime one lately about art heists. It's crazy how people can just walk out of a museum with a painting worth millions of dollars. Like, don't they have security cameras? I bought a cheap security camera off Amazon for my apartment once because I thought someone was stealing my Amazon packages. Turned out it was just the delivery guy leaving them at the wrong door down the hall. My neighbor was so confused when he kept finding boxes of protein powder and cat food on his welcome mat. I don't even have a cat. I just buy the food to feed the strays behind my building...\n\nBut yeah, I don't feel forced to get up, exactly. I just do it, even when I know I really need to stay in my seat and work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yeah, I think I kind of mentioned this a few minutes ago, but that's exactly what happens. If I open up a file and see that it's going to need a really long written explanation, or if I have to cross-check a bunch of dense policy language, I just completely put it off. I just have to get up.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your workstation because you absolutely have to, or is it more that you just really want to?\n\nPatient: I don't know if \"absolutely have to\" is the right way to put it. It's not like a life-or-death feeling, you know? It's more like... I just really, really want to. Like this itch in my brain that says \"okay, time to stand up now.\" It's kind of like when you're watching a movie and you realize you forgot to take the laundry out of the dryer, and you know it's going to get all wrinkled if you don't do it right then. \n\nSpeaking of laundry, I tried this new detergent last week. It was supposed to smell like \"Mountain Rain\" or something, but it honestly just smells like those little pine tree air fresheners people hang in their cars. My dad used to have like five of those hanging from his rearview mirror at once in his old Chevy truck. He refused to get rid of that truck, even when the passenger side door wouldn't open from the inside anymore. You had to roll down the window and use the outside handle if you wanted to get out. We took it to a drive-in movie theater once, which was super cool. Have you ever been to a drive-in? There's hardly any left around here. They always had the best popcorn, way better than regular theaters. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, getting up from my desk. Yeah, I wouldn't say I *have* to, like I'm being forced. I just really want to, and then before I know it, I'm already walking away from my desk.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your workstation to do something specific, or is it just to move around in general?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really to do anything specific. I just kind of... walk. Like, I don't have a destination in mind most of the time. I'll just end up wandering over by the big windows near the elevators. They're those massive floor-to-ceiling ones, you know? They actually had to put these little frosted bird silhouettes on them a few weeks ago because birds kept flying straight into the glass. It was happening like twice a week. Which is super sad, obviously, but the sound it makes is surprisingly loud. It echoes through the whole floor. \n\nMy grandma used to have this parakeet named Petey when I was a kid, and he would always fly right into her sliding glass door if she forgot to close the curtains. He was usually fine, just kind of dazed, but he loved eating those little millet spray things... man, those things make a huge mess. She was always vacuuming birdseed off the rug. She had this really old Hoover vacuum that weighed like fifty pounds and sounded like a jet engine taking off. \n\nBut, uh... yeah, I don't get up to do a task or anything. Just to move around, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you can't control?\n\nPatient: I mean, I *can* control it, I think. It's not like my legs move on their own or anything. It's just... really hard not to. It feels like if I try to force myself to stay seated, the urge just gets stronger and stronger until I finally give in. It's sort of like when you're trying to hold your breath underwater. \n\nI used to do that all the time when I was little at the community pool. We'd have these contests to see who could stay under the longest. My friend Tyler always won because he took swimming lessons at the Y. He even learned how to do that flip turn thing at the end of the lane. I tried it once and ended up just kicking the wall super hard and bruising my heel. I couldn't wear regular shoes for like a week, I had to wear these weird foam sandals my mom bought at the pharmacy. They were bright orange, which was super embarrassing for a middle schooler. The pharmacy we went to was one of those old-school ones that still had a soda fountain in the back, though. They made the best cherry phosphates. \n\nBut yeah, wait, control. Right. I wouldn't say I *can't* control it, it's just... really uncomfortable if I try to ignore it, so I just end up getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you feel compelled to, rather than just because you want to?\n\nPatient: I guess \"compelled\" is probably a good word for it. Honestly, that's actually the main thing that pushed me to come in to see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, over and over, even when I know I’m absolutely supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI process insurance policies and claims, and man, some of the stuff people submit is wild. Just the other day I had a claim where a guy said a wild turkey shattered his windshield. A whole turkey! I didn't even know turkeys could fly that high. I always thought they just kind of hopped around on the ground, like chickens. My uncle had chickens when I was growing up—Rhode Island Reds, I think they were called. They laid these really cool brown eggs, and my aunt used to make the best quiche out of them. She’d put spinach and this really fancy gruyere cheese in it. \n\nI tried to make a quiche myself once a couple years ago, but I didn't blind-bake the crust first, so it got super soggy on the bottom. Have you ever watched the Great British Baking Show? They always talk about getting a \"soggy bottom.\" Paul Hollywood would have been so disappointed in me. It's such a relaxing show to have on in the background, though. The tent they bake in just looks so nice, especially when it's raining outside. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, feeling compelled. Yeah, I know I need to sit there and do the reviews, but I just keep getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your desk out of habit or because you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's a bit of both, but mostly it feels like I just have to. Like I said before, that's really the whole reason I finally made an appointment. I just... I keep getting up from my desk. All day. Even when I have a stack of claim reviews that absolutely have to get done, and I know I'm supposed to just stay put and finish them. \n\nIt's actually a pretty strict metric we have to hit for the reviews, they track our numbers every week. My supervisor, Brenda, she sends out this spreadsheet every Friday afternoon. She color-codes it, too, which I always thought was a little passive-aggressive. Like, if you're in the red, everyone knows. She uses this really bright, aggressive red, too, not like a soft pink or anything. It reminds me of my old high school English teacher, Mr. Harrison. He used to grade our essays with this thick red marker that bled through the paper. He was a tough grader, but he actually gave me a B+ on my final paper about *The Great Gatsby*. I always liked that book. The whole green light at the end of the dock thing... it's just really good symbolism, you know? They made a movie out of it a few years ago with Leonardo DiCaprio. He was really good in it, but I think I prefer him in *Inception*. That movie completely messed with my head. The spinning top at the end? So frustrating. I watched it three times trying to figure out if he was still dreaming or not. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... yeah. It's not just a habit. I know I'm supposed to stay at my workstation and do the work, but I just keep getting up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any specific thoughts or urges in your mind when you feel the need to get up from your desk?\n\nPatient: Not really any specific thoughts, no. Like, I'm not thinking \"I need to get up to go check the mail\" or \"I need to get up to stretch.\" It's just... this physical restlessness. And like I was saying, that's really the main thing that pushed me to come in. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI've tried bringing a water bottle to my desk so I don't have an excuse to go to the breakroom, but then I just end up getting up to go to the bathroom more often. I bought one of those really big insulated ones, the kind that holds like half a gallon. It's blue, like a really bright cobalt blue. I dropped it in the parking lot last week and dented the bottom, which is super annoying because now it wobbles when I set it down. It makes this metallic clinking sound every time I bump my desk. My desk is one of those standing desks, actually, but the motor broke on it like three months ago so it's stuck in the sitting position. Maintenance keeps saying they're going to come fix it, but you know how that goes. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always telling these really long stories about his RV trips. He went to Mount Rushmore last summer and apparently got a flat tire right in front of George Washington's face. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really have a specific thought in my head when it happens. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there doing my reviews, and I just can't seem to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk makes you feel better or more comfortable?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe a little bit? It's not like it's a huge relief or anything, but it does relieve that restless feeling for a second. But honestly, like I've been saying, it's just really frustrating. It's the whole reason I'm here. I just keep getting up from my workstation, over and over, even when I know I have to stay put and get those claim reviews done. \n\nIt's actually kind of embarrassing when other people notice. My coworker, Sarah, sits in the cubicle next to mine. She has this huge collection of succulents on her desk. Like, an aggressive amount of plants. I don't know how she keeps them all alive, especially with the weird fluorescent lighting in our office. I bought a cactus once from IKEA and managed to kill it in a month. I think I overwatered it. Did you know you're only supposed to water them like once a month? I was giving it a little drink every day like it was a regular houseplant. It just kind of turned to mush. IKEA has really good Swedish meatballs, though. I usually go just to eat at the cafeteria and don't even buy any furniture. The lingonberry jam is surprisingly good with the gravy. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know if getting up makes me feel *better*, exactly. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there working, and I can't.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you would feel anxious or uncomfortable if you didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do feel a little uncomfortable if I try to force myself to stay sitting. It's like this tension builds up. But honestly, like I keep saying, this is exactly what pushed me to come see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even though I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nIt’s just so hard to focus when my chair is so uncomfortable, too. It’s one of those mesh ones, but the lumbar support piece snapped off. I tried taping it back on with duct tape—the silver kind, not the black kind. Did you know they make duct tape in all sorts of patterns now? I saw some at the hardware store the other day that looked like galaxy print. My niece would love that, she's obsessed with space right now. She wants to be an astronaut. Or a veterinarian for space dogs, I'm not really sure which. She's only six, so her career plans change every week. Last week she wanted to be a mermaid princess. We watched *The Little Mermaid* together, the animated one, not the new live-action one. The music in that movie is just so catchy. \"Under the Sea\" was stuck in my head for three days straight. \n\nBut anyway, yeah... And like I was saying, that's really the main thing that pushed me to come in. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI've tried bringing a water bottle to my desk so I don't have an excuse to go to the breakroom, but then I just end up getting up to go to the bathroom more often. I bought one of those really big insulated ones, the kind that holds like half a gallon. It's blue, like a really bright cobalt blue. I dropped it in the parking lot last week and dented the bottom, which is super annoying because now it wobbles when I set it down. It makes this metallic clinking sound every time I bump my desk. My desk is one of those standing desks, actually, but the motor broke on it like three months ago so it's stuck in the sitting position. Maintenance keeps saying they're going to come fix it, but you know how that goes. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always telling these really long stories about his RV trips. He went to Mount Rushmore last summer and apparently got a flat tire right in front of George Washington's face. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really have a specific thought in my head when it happens. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there doing my reviews, and I just can't seem to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk makes you feel better or more comfortable?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe a little bit? It's the whole reason I'm here. I just keep getting up from my workstation, over and over, even when I know I have to stay put and get those claim reviews done. \n\nIt's actually kind of embarrassing when other people notice. I mean, yeah, I guess so. Like I said before, that's really the main reason I even made this appointment. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I'm supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. It's like I just have to walk away.

Like, I’ll just be wandering around the office even when I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. I just can't seem to stay in my chair when I need to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel restless when you're sitting at your workstation?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess you could say that. Like, if I'm stuck in one of those long team meetings or we have to do a training session, I'm always bouncing my leg or clicking my pen. Just shifting around in my chair a lot, even when I'm really trying to look like I'm paying attention. Actually, my pen clicking drives my supervisor crazy. It vibrates right through the wall where my TV is. But yeah, no, I don't really feel that restless feeling when I'm not at the office.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious when you're sitting at your workstation?\n\nPatient: Anxious? No, I wouldn't say anxious. It's not like I'm worried about anything specific, you know? Well, except maybe for the vending machine in the breakroom. They restocked it last week with those jalapeno chips I like, but the coil on the C4 slot is totally busted. I watched this guy from marketing lose a dollar-fifty to it on Monday. We took it to a drive-in movie theater once, which was super cool. Have you ever been to a drive-in? There's hardly any left around here. They always had the best popcorn, way better than regular theaters. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, getting up from my desk. Yeah, I wouldn't say I *have* to, like I'm being forced. I just really want to, and then before I know it, I'm already walking away from my desk.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your workstation to do something specific, or is it just to move around in general?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really to do anything specific. I just kind of... walk. Like, I don't have a destination in mind most of the time. I'll just end up wandering over by the big windows near the elevators. They're those massive floor-to-ceiling ones, you know? They actually had to put these little frosted bird silhouettes on them a few weeks ago because birds kept flying straight into the glass. It was happening like twice a week. Which is super sad, obviously, but the sound it makes is surprisingly loud. It echoes through the whole floor. \n\nMy grandma used to have this parakeet named Petey when I was a kid, and he would always fly right into her sliding glass door if she forgot to close the curtains. He was usually fine, just kind of dazed, but he loved eating those little millet spray things... man, those things make a huge mess. She was always vacuuming birdseed off the rug. She had this really old Hoover vacuum that weighed like fifty pounds and sounded like a jet engine taking off. \n\nBut, uh... yeah, I don't get up to do a task or anything. Just to move around, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you can't control?\n\nPatient: I mean, I *can* control it, I think. It's not like my legs move on their own or anything. It's just... really hard not to. It feels like if I try to force myself to stay seated, the urge just gets stronger and stronger until I finally give in. It's sort of like when you're trying to hold your breath underwater. \n\nI used to do that all the time when I was little at the community pool. We'd have these contests to see who could stay under the longest. My friend Tyler always won because he took swimming lessons at the Y. He even learned how to do that flip turn thing at the end of the lane. I tried it once and ended up just kicking the wall super hard and bruising my heel. I couldn't wear regular shoes for like a week, I had to wear these weird foam sandals my mom bought at the pharmacy. They were bright orange, which was super embarrassing for a middle schooler. The pharmacy we went to was one of those old-school ones that still had a soda fountain in the back, though. They made the best cherry phosphates. \n\nBut yeah, wait, control. Right. I wouldn't say I *can't* control it, it's just... really uncomfortable if I try to ignore it, so I just end up getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you feel compelled to, rather than just because you want to?\n\nPatient: I guess \"compelled\" is probably a good word for it. Honestly, that's actually the main thing that pushed me to come in to see you in the first place. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, over and over, even when I know I’m absolutely supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI process insurance policies and claims, and man, some of the stuff people submit is wild. Just the other day I had a claim where a guy said a wild turkey shattered his windshield. A whole turkey! I didn't even know turkeys could fly that high. I always thought they just kind of hopped around on the ground, like chickens. My uncle had chickens when I was growing up—Rhode Island Reds, I think they were called. They laid these really cool brown eggs, and my aunt used to make the best quiche out of them. She’d put spinach and this really fancy gruyere cheese in it. \n\nI tried to make a quiche myself once a couple years ago, but I didn't blind-bake the crust first, so it got super soggy on the bottom. Have you ever watched the Great British Baking Show? They always talk about getting a \"soggy bottom.\" Paul Hollywood would have been so disappointed in me. It's such a relaxing show to have on in the background, though. The tent they bake in just looks so nice, especially when it's raining outside. \n\nBut, um... what was the question? Oh, right, feeling compelled. Yeah, I know I need to sit there and do the reviews, but I just keep getting up anyway.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up from your desk out of habit or because you feel like you have to do it?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess it's a bit of both, but mostly it feels like I just have to. Like I said before, that's really the whole reason I finally made an appointment. I just... I keep getting up from my desk. All day. Even when I have a stack of claim reviews that absolutely have to get done, and I know I'm supposed to just stay put and finish them. \n\nIt's actually a pretty strict metric we have to hit for the reviews, they track our numbers every week. My supervisor, Brenda, she sends out this spreadsheet every Friday afternoon. She color-codes it, too, which I always thought was a little passive-aggressive. Like, if you're in the red, everyone knows. She uses this really bright, aggressive red, too, not like a soft pink or anything. It reminds me of my old high school English teacher, Mr. Harrison. He used to grade our essays with this thick red marker that bled through the paper. He was a tough grader, but he actually gave me a B+ on my final paper about *The Great Gatsby*. I always liked that book. The whole green light at the end of the dock thing... it's just really good symbolism, you know? They made a movie out of it a few years ago with Leonardo DiCaprio. He was really good in it, but I think I prefer him in *Inception*. That movie completely messed with my head. The spinning top at the end? So frustrating. I watched it three times trying to figure out if he was still dreaming or not. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... yeah. It's not just a habit. I know I'm supposed to stay at my workstation and do the work, but I just keep getting up.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any specific thoughts or urges in your mind when you feel the need to get up from your desk?\n\nPatient: Not really any specific thoughts, no. Like, I'm not thinking \"I need to get up to go check the mail\" or \"I need to get up to stretch.\" It's just... this physical restlessness. I just keep getting up from my workstation during the day, even when I know I’m supposed to stay put and finish my claim reviews. \n\nI've tried bringing a water bottle to my desk so I don't have an excuse to go to the breakroom, but then I just end up getting up to go to the bathroom more often. I bought one of those really big insulated ones, the kind that holds like half a gallon. It's blue, like a really bright cobalt blue. I dropped it in the parking lot last week and dented the bottom, which is super annoying because now it wobbles when I set it down. It makes this metallic clinking sound every time I bump my desk. My desk is one of those standing desks, actually, but the motor broke on it like three months ago so it's stuck in the sitting position. Maintenance keeps saying they're going to come fix it, but you know how that goes. The maintenance guy, Frank, he's always telling these really long stories about his RV trips. He went to Mount Rushmore last summer and apparently got a flat tire right in front of George Washington's face. \n\nBut yeah... I don't really have a specific thought in my head when it happens. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there doing my reviews, and I just can't seem to stay put.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk makes you feel better or more comfortable?\n\nPatient: I mean, maybe a little bit? It's the whole reason I'm here. I just keep getting up from my workstation, over and over, even when I know I have to stay put and get those claim reviews done. \n\nIt's actually kind of embarrassing when other people notice. My coworker, Sarah, sits in the cubicle next to mine. She has this huge collection of succulents on her desk. Like, an aggressive amount of plants. I don't know how she keeps them all alive, especially with the weird fluorescent lighting in our office. I bought a cactus once from IKEA and managed to kill it in a month. I think I overwatered it. Did you know you're only supposed to water them like once a month? I was giving it a little drink every day like it was a regular houseplant. It just kind of turned to mush. IKEA has really good Swedish meatballs, though. I usually go just to eat at the cafeteria and don't even buy any furniture. I just know I'm supposed to be sitting there working, and I can't.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like getting up from your desk is something you do because you would feel anxious or uncomfortable if you didn't?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess I do feel a little uncomfortable if I try to force myself to stay sitting. My niece would love that, she's obsessed with space right now. She wants to be an astronaut. Or a veterinarian for space dogs, I'm not really sure which. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, that's exactly it. It's like my brain just can't hold onto what I'm looking at. If I'm trying to read through a file and the people in the cubicle next to me start talking about some other claim, or if I suddenly remember I need to pick up dry cleaning... my attention just completely slides right off the page. The dry cleaners I go to is actually right next to this amazing taco truck. They make these al pastor tacos with the little chunks of pineapple in them, which I know sounds weird, but the sweet and salty combination is incredible. They only take cash, though, which is super annoying because who carries cash anymore? I always have to stop at the ATM at the gas station on the corner first, and they charge you like four dollars just to withdraw your own money. It feels like a scam. I read an article once about how ATMs are actually really easy for people to put those card skimmers on. Now I always wiggle the card reader thing before I put my debit card in. The guy behind me in line the other day looked at me like I was crazy when I was yanking on it, but you can never be too careful. My roommate got his card skimmed once and someone bought like three hundred dollars worth of stuff at a pet store in Ohio. We live in Texas, so it obviously wasn't him. Plus, he's allergic to dogs... 

But anyway, yeah, the focus thing. Once my attention slips because of a conversation or a random thought, I just can't get it back, and that's usually when I end up getting up from my desk.
